Remarks.&mdash;During April the highest reading of the barometer was 30.350, on the 6th; and the lowest 29.730, on the 21st. The highest temperature was 88&deg;, on the 25th; and the lowest 55&deg;, on the 7th. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 5th, averaging 10.4 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 15 days, measuring 6.09 inches. During May the highest reading of the barometer was 30.190, on the 4th; and the lowest 29.780, on the 25th. The highest temperature was 930, on the 17th; and the lowest 62&deg;, on the 3rd. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 2nd, averaging 13.8 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 20 days, measuring 8.33 inches. During June the highest reading of the barometer was 30.140, on the 9th; and the lowest 29.780, on the 1st. The highest temperature was 95&deg;.s, on the 10th; and the lowest 66&deg;, on the 2nd. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the ist, averaging 11.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 18 days, measuring 7.83 inches. IX. 166 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, No. 267. YANGTZE RIVER&mdash;KIUKIANG DISTRICT. N.E. CROSSING BEACON LIGHT. Notice is hereby given that the N.E. Crossing Beacon Light has been shifted 1,498 yards S. 790 E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. Shanghai, 26th April 1893. No. 268. YANGTZE RIVER&mdash;KIUKIANG DISTRICT. FITZROY ISLAND BEACON LIGHT. Notice is hereby given that the Fitzroy Island Beacon Light has been shifted 130 yards N. 730 E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. Shanghai, 2nd June 1893. No. 269. CANTON DISTRICT. WAGLAN ISLAND LIGHTHOUSE. Notice is hereby given that the light on Waglan Island was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 10th ultimo. The illuminating apparatus is revolving dioptric of the first order, showing double white flashes at intervals of half a minute. The lighthouse stands on the summit of the island, and the light, which is elevated 225 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clear weather at a distance of 22 nautical miles in all directions where it is not obscured by land. The tower is round, of iron, 25 feet high, with a total height from its base to the lantern vane of 52 feet.
1893.] NOTIFICATIONS. 167 The lower half of the tower is painted white, the upper half red, and the lantern white. The dwellings are white. Approximate position:&mdash; Latitude 220 ii&#39; 18&quot; N. Longitude 1140 18&#39; i&quot;E. Fog Gun Signal. The keepers at this station on hearing a bell, fog horn, steam whistle, or any other sound, during foggy or thick weather, indicating the proximity of a vessel, will fire two guns with an interval of fifteen seconds between them, and, if the vessel&#39;s fog signal&mdash;showing that she is under way&mdash;continues to be heard, will repeat the firing after an interval of twelve minutes. Shanghai, 5th June 1893. N.B.&mdash;When the light on Waglan was first exhibited it showed only 30 per cent, of its full power between the bearings, from seaward, of N. by E. round by east to S.E. by S. It now shows to its full poAver in all directions. No. 270. CANTON DISTRICT. NEWLY-DISCOVERED ROCK BETWEEN WAGLAN AND SUNKONG ISLANDS&mdash;PU TOY GROUP. Notice is hereby given that a rock, with 4 fathoms on it at low water, has been found in the channel between Waglan and Sunkong Islands, at a distance of about if cables to the westward of the reef on the west side of South Waglan. From the rock the north point of North Waglan bears N. 470 E., magnetic, and the south point of South Waglan S. 710 E., magnetic. Shanghai, 12th June 1893.

1893.] LAPPA. 207 (Received 17 th Jtdy 1893.J CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 1 ith July 1893. SIR, I.&mdash;a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the second quarter of the year 1893, April-June. b. The Custom House 4b til) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium {Hk.Th. 30 and Hk.Th 80 per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com&not; menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow (,% and Chienshan (fly fXf) stations, with three sub-stations, known as the Shekkok (%j Kuttai (~n ife), and Kwanchiap (ID pp]) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II. MOVEMENTS OF VESSELS.&mdash;The number and movements of vessels, inwards and outwards, during the quarter, are set forth in the following Tables:&mdash; i&deg;. MALOWCHOW. JUNKS. April. May. June.
